</li>
<li id="ca-history" class="mw-list-item vector-menu-item--collapsible vector-tab-noicon"><a data-mw-interface  href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=history" title="Past revisions of this page [h]" accesskey="h" class=""><span>View history</span></a>
</li>

			
		</ul>
		
	</div>
</div>

				<div id="bodyContent" class="vector-body" aria-labelledby="firstHeading" data-mw-ve-target-container>
					<div class="vector-body-before-content">
							<div class="mw-indicators">
		</div>

						<div id="siteSub" class="noprint">From Wikipedia, the free encyclopedia</div>
					</div>
					<div id="contentSub"><div id="mw-content-subtitle"></div></div>
					
					
					<div id="mw-content-text" class="mw-body-content"><div class="mw-subjectpageheader">
</div><div class="mw-content-ltr mw-parser-output" lang="en" dir="ltr" data-mw-parsoid-version="0.24.0.0-alpha15" data-mw-html-version="2.8.0"><section data-mw-section-id="0" id="mwAQ"><div class="shortdescription nomobile noexcerpt noprint searchaux" style="display:none" about="#mwt1" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Short description","href":"./Template:Short_description"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"AI-dependent computer programming"}},"i":0}}]}'>AI-dependent computer programming</div><span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt1"><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Articles_with_short_description"/><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Short_description_is_different_from_Wikidata"/></span>
<p id="mwAw"><span typeof="mw:Nowiki mw:Transclusion" about="#mwt2" id="mwBA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Use dmy dates","href":"./Template:Use_dmy_dates"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"February 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'></span><link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Use_dmy_dates_from_February_2026" about="#mwt2" id="mwBQ"/>
<b id="mwBg">Vibe coding</b> is <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AI-assisted_software_development" title="AI-assisted software development" id="mwBw">software development assisted by artificial intelligence</a> (AI) where the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software" title="Software" id="mwCA">software</a> developer describes a project or task in a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AI_prompt" title="AI prompt" class="mw-redirect" id="mwCQ">prompt</a> to a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Large_language_model" title="Large language model" id="mwCg">large language model</a> (LLM) which <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Automatic_programming" title="Automatic programming" id="mwCw">generates source code automatically</a>. Vibe coding may involve accepting AI-generated code without thorough review of the output, instead relying on results and follow-up prompts to guide changes.<sup about="#mwt5" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Edwards-2025_1-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Edwards-2025"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Edwards-2025-1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Edwards-2025-1" id="mwDA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwDQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwDg">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwDw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt8" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-The_Times_of_India-2025_2-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"The Times of India-2025"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-The_Times_of_India-2025-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-The_Times_of_India-2025-2" id="mwEA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwEQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwEg">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwEw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwFA">The term was coined in February 2025 by computer scientist <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Karpathy" title="Andrej Karpathy" id="mwFQ">Andrej Karpathy</a>, a co-founder of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenAI" title="OpenAI" id="mwFg">OpenAI</a> and former AI leader at <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tesla,_Inc." title="Tesla, Inc." id="mwFw">Tesla</a>. <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Merriam-Webster" title="Merriam-Webster" id="mwGA">Merriam-Webster</a> listed the term in March 2025 as a "slang &amp; trending" expression.<sup about="#mwt11" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-merriam-webster_3-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"merriam-webster"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-merriam-webster-3"}}'><a href="#cite_note-merriam-webster-3" id="mwGQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwGg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwGw">[</span>3<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwHA">]</span></span></a></sup> It was named the <i id="mwHQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Collins_English_Dictionary" title="Collins English Dictionary" id="mwHg">Collins English Dictionary</a></i> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Word_of_the_year#Collins_English_Dictionary" title="Word of the year" id="mwHw">Word of the Year</a> for 2025.<sup about="#mwt14" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-4"}}'><a href="#cite_note-4" id="mwIA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwIQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIg">[</span>4<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwIw">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt17" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-5"}}'><a href="#cite_note-5" id="mwJA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwJQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwJg">[</span>5<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwJw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwKA">Advocates of vibe coding say that it allows even <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/End-user_computing" title="End-user computing" id="mwKQ">amateur programmers</a> to produce software without the extensive training and skills required for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_engineering" title="Software engineering" id="mwKg">software engineering</a>.<sup about="#mwt20" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-6"}}'><a href="#cite_note-6" id="mwKw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwLA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLQ">[</span>6<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwLg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt23" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Chowdhury-2025_7-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Chowdhury-2025"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Chowdhury-2025-7"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Chowdhury-2025-7" id="mwLw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwMA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMQ">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwMg">]</span></span></a></sup> Critics point out a lack of accountability, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_maintenance#Maintainability" title="Software maintenance" id="mwMw">maintainability</a>, and an increased risk of introducing <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_vulnerabilities" title="Security vulnerabilities" class="mw-redirect" id="mwNA">security vulnerabilities</a> in the resulting software.<sup about="#mwt24" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Edwards-2025_1-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Edwards-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Edwards-2025-1" id="mwNQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwNg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwNw">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt25" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Chowdhury-2025_7-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Chowdhury-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Chowdhury-2025-7" id="mwOQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwOg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwOw">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwPA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwPQ" data-mw='{"autoGenerated":true}'/></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwPg" aria-labelledby="Definition"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Definition">Definition</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Definition"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwPw"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Andrej_Karpathy,_OpenAI.png" class="mw-file-description" id="mwQA"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Andrej_Karpathy,_OpenAI.png" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c0/Andrej_Karpathy%2C_OpenAI.png/250px-Andrej_Karpathy%2C_OpenAI.png" decoding="async" data-file-width="490" data-file-height="665" data-file-type="bitmap" height="339" width="250" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c0/Andrej_Karpathy%2C_OpenAI.png 2x" class="mw-file-element" id="mwQQ"/></a><figcaption id="mwQg">The term "vibe coding" was coined by AI researcher <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Karpathy" title="Andrej Karpathy" id="mwQw">Andrej Karpathy</a> (pictured in 2019).</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwRA">The concept refers to a coding approach that relies on LLMs, allowing programmers to generate working code by providing natural language descriptions rather than manually writing in a formal programming language.<sup about="#mwt26" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Edwards-2025_1-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Edwards-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Edwards-2025-1" id="mwRQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwRg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwRw">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt27" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-The_Times_of_India-2025_2-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"The Times of India-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-The_Times_of_India-2025-2" id="mwSQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwSg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwSw">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTA">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt28" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Chowdhury-2025_7-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Chowdhury-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Chowdhury-2025-7" id="mwTQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwTg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwTw">[</span>7<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwUA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwUQ">Karpathy described it as a form of coding where you "fully give in to the vibes, embrace exponentials, and forget that the code even exists".<sup about="#mwt31" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Karpathy-X-post_8-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Karpathy-X-post"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Karpathy-X-post-8"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Karpathy-X-post-8" id="mwUg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwUw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVA">[</span>8<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVQ">]</span></span></a></sup> When vibe coding, the programmer guides, tests, and gives feedback about the AI-generated <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Source_code" title="Source code" id="mwVg">source code</a>, rather than manually writing code.<sup about="#mwt32" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Edwards-2025_1-3" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Edwards-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Edwards-2025-1" id="mwVw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwWA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWQ">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwWg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt33" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-The_Times_of_India-2025_2-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"The Times of India-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-The_Times_of_India-2025-2" id="mwWw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwXA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXQ">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwXg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt36" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Roose-2025_9-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Roose-2025"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Roose-2025-9"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Roose-2025-9" id="mwXw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwYA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYQ">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwYg">]</span></span></a></sup>
The concept of vibe coding elaborates on Karpathy's claim from 2023 that "the hottest new <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Programming_language" title="Programming language" id="mwYw">programming language</a> is English", meaning that the capabilities of LLMs were such that humans would no longer need to learn specific programming languages to command computers.<sup about="#mwt39" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Naughton-2025_10-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Naughton-2025"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-Naughton-2025-10"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Naughton-2025-10" id="mwZA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwZQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZg">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwZw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwaA">Some commentators argue that a key to the definition is a lack of knowledge about the code, and that thorough review and testing is incompatible with the definition of vibe coding.<sup about="#mwt40" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Edwards-2025_1-4" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Edwards-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Edwards-2025-1" id="mwaQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwag"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwaw">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwbA">]</span></span></a></sup> Programmer <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simon_Willison" title="Simon Willison" id="mwbQ">Simon Willison</a> said: "If an LLM wrote every line of your code, but you've reviewed, tested, and understood it all, that's not vibe coding in my book—that's using an LLM as a typing assistant."<sup about="#mwt41" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Edwards-2025_1-5" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Edwards-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Edwards-2025-1" id="mwbg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwbw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcA">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwcQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwcg" aria-labelledby="Reception_and_use"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Reception_and_use">Reception and use</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Reception and use"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwcw">In February 2025, <i id="mwdA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_New_York_Times" title="The New York Times" id="mwdQ">New York Times</a></i> journalist <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Roose" title="Kevin Roose" id="mwdg">Kevin Roose</a>, who is not a professional coder, experimented with vibe coding to create several small-scale applications. He described these as "software for one" due to the ability to personalize the software. However, Roose also stated that the results are often limited and prone to errors.<sup about="#mwt42" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Roose-2025_9-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Roose-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Roose-2025-9" id="mwdw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mweA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mweQ">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mweg">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt43" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Naughton-2025_10-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Naughton-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Naughton-2025-10" id="mwew"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwfA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfQ">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwfg">]</span></span></a></sup> In one case, the AI-generated code fabricated fake reviews for an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-commerce" title="E-commerce" id="mwfw">e-commerce</a> site.<sup about="#mwt44" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Roose-2025_9-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Roose-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Roose-2025-9" id="mwgA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwgQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgg">[</span>9<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwgw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwhA">In response to Roose, cognitive scientist <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gary_Marcus" title="Gary Marcus" id="mwhQ">Gary Marcus</a> said that the algorithm that generated Roose's LunchBox Buddy app had presumably been trained on existing code for similar tasks. Marcus said that Roose's enthusiasm stemmed from reproduction, not originality.<sup about="#mwt45" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Naughton-2025_10-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Naughton-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Naughton-2025-10" id="mwhg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwhw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiA">[</span>10<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwig">In March 2025, <i id="mwiw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Y_Combinator" title="Y Combinator" id="mwjA">Y Combinator</a></i> reported that 25% of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Startup_companies" title="Startup companies" class="mw-redirect" id="mwjQ">startup companies</a> in its Winter 2025 batch had codebases that were 95% AI-generated.<sup about="#mwt48" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-tc_mehta_11-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"tc_mehta"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-tc_mehta-11"}}'><a href="#cite_note-tc_mehta-11" id="mwjg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwjw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkA">[</span>11<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwkQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwkg">Inspired by "vibe coding", <i id="mwkw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Economist" title="The Economist" id="mwlA">The Economist</a></i> suggested the term "vibe valuation" to describe the very large <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valuation_(finance)" title="Valuation (finance)" id="mwlQ">valuations</a> of AI startups by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venture_capital" title="Venture capital" id="mwlg">venture capital</a> firms that ignore accepted metrics such as <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annual_recurring_revenue" title="Annual recurring revenue" class="mw-redirect" id="mwlw">annual recurring revenue</a>.<sup about="#mwt51" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-12" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-12"}}'><a href="#cite_note-12" id="mwmA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwmQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmg">[</span>12<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwmw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwnA">In June 2025, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Ng" title="Andrew Ng" id="mwnQ">Andrew Ng</a> took issue with the term, saying that it misleads people into assuming that software engineers just "go with the vibes" when using AI tools to create applications.<sup about="#mwt54" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-13" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-13"}}'><a href="#cite_note-13" id="mwng"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwnw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwoA">[</span>13<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwoQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwog">In July 2025, <i id="mwow"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Wall_Street_Journal" title="The Wall Street Journal" id="mwpA">The Wall Street Journal</a></i> reported that vibe coding was being adopted by professional software engineers for commercial use cases.<sup about="#mwt57" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-14" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-14"}}'><a href="#cite_note-14" id="mwpQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwpg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwpw">[</span>14<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwqA">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwqQ">It was reported in January 2026 that <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linus_Torvalds" title="Linus Torvalds" id="mwqg">Linus Torvalds</a> had made use of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Google_Antigravity" title="Google Antigravity" id="mwqw">Google Antigravity</a> to vibe code a visualization tool for his AudioNoise random digital audio effects generator. Torvalds explained in the project's <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/README" title="README" id="mwrA">README file</a> that "the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Python_(programming_language)" title="Python (programming language)" id="mwrQ">Python</a> visualizer tool has been basically written by vibe-coding".<sup about="#mwt60" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-15" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-15"}}'><a href="#cite_note-15" id="mwrg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwrw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsA">[</span>15<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwsQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt63" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-16" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-16"}}'><a href="#cite_note-16" id="mwsg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwsw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtA">[</span>16<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwtQ">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mwtg" aria-labelledby="Criticism"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Criticism">Criticism</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Criticism"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<section data-mw-section-id="4" id="mwtw" aria-labelledby="Quality_of_code_and_security_issues"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Quality_of_code_and_security_issues">Quality of code and security issues</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Quality of code and security issues"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwuA">Vibe coding has raised concerns about understanding and accountability. Some developers commit AI-generated code without comprehending its functionality, leading to undetected bugs, errors, or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Security_vulnerability" title="Security vulnerability" class="mw-redirect" id="mwuQ">security vulnerabilities</a>.<sup about="#mwt66" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-17" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-17"}}'><a href="#cite_note-17" id="mwug"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwuw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvA">[</span>17<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwvQ">]</span></span></a></sup> While this approach may be suitable for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Software_prototyping" title="Software prototyping" id="mwvg">prototyping</a> or "throwaway weekend projects" as Karpathy originally envisioned, it is considered by some experts to pose risks in professional settings, where a deep understanding of the code is necessary for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debugging" title="Debugging" id="mwvw">debugging</a>, maintenance, and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_security" title="Computer security" id="mwwA">security</a>. <i id="mwwQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ars_Technica" title="Ars Technica" id="mwwg">Ars Technica</a></i> cites Simon Willison, who stated: "Vibe coding your way to a production codebase is clearly risky. Most of the work we do as software engineers involves evolving existing systems, where the quality and understandability of the underlying code is crucial."<sup about="#mwt67" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-Edwards-2025_1-6" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"Edwards-2025"}}'><a href="#cite_note-Edwards-2025-1" id="mwww"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwxA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxQ">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwxg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwxw">In May 2025, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lovable_(company)" title="Lovable (company)" id="mwyA">Lovable</a>, a Swedish vibe coding app, was reported to have security vulnerabilities in the code it generated, with 170 out of 1,645 Lovable-created <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Web_application" title="Web application" id="mwyQ">web applications</a> having an issue that would allow personal information to be accessed by anyone.<sup about="#mwt70" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-18" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-18"}}'><a href="#cite_note-18" id="mwyg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwyw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwzA">[</span>18<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwzQ">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt73" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-19" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-19"}}'><a href="#cite_note-19" id="mwzg"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwzw"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0A">[</span>19<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw0Q">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw0g">In October 2025 Veracode released a study that showed that over the last 3 years LLMs had become dramatically better at generating functional code, but that the security of generated code had generally not improved. Moreover, larger models were not better than small ones at generating secure code. There was a small increase in security from the OpenAI reasoning models, but not in other reasoning models, and this increase was nothing like the improvement in generated functionality.<sup about="#mwt76" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-20" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-20"}}'><a href="#cite_note-20" id="mw0w"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw1A"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1Q">[</span>20<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw1g">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw1w">In December 2025, computer security researcher Etizaz Mohsin discovered a security flaw in the Orchids vibe coding platform, which he demonstrated to a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BBC_News" title="BBC News" id="mw2A">BBC News</a> reporter in February 2026.<sup about="#mwt79" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-21" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-21"}}'><a href="#cite_note-21" id="mw2Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw2g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw2w">[</span>21<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw3A">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw3Q">A December 2025 analysis by CodeRabbit of 470 open-source <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GitHub" title="GitHub" id="mw3g">GitHub</a> pull requests found that code that was co-authored by generative AI contained approximately 1.7 times more "major" issues compared to human-written code. The study revealed that AI co-authored code showed elevated rates of logic errors, including incorrect dependencies, flawed <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Control_flow" title="Control flow" id="mw3w">control flow</a>, misconfigurations (75% more common), and security vulnerabilities (2.74x higher). Additionally, they also reported high code readability issues, including formatting errors and naming inconsistencies.<sup about="#mwt82" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-22" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-22"}}'><a href="#cite_note-22" id="mw4A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw4Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw4g">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw4w">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt85" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:1_23-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":1"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:1-23"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:1-23" id="mw5A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw5Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw5g">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw5w">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw6A">In July 2025, SaaStr founder documented his negative experiences with vibe coding: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Replit" title="Replit" id="mw6Q">Replit</a>'s AI agent deleted a database despite explicit instructions not to make any changes.<sup about="#mwt88" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-24" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-24"}}'><a href="#cite_note-24" id="mw6g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw6w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7A">[</span>24<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw7Q">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt92" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-25" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-25"}}'><a href="#cite_note-25" id="mw7g"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw7w"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8A">[</span>25<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw8Q">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mw8g">When <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rsync" title="Rsync" id="mw8w">rsync</a> was updated to 3.4.3 on May 20, 2026, several users reported being unable to perform incremental file backups. Those users found that since rsync 3.4.1, dozens of git commits have been performed by "tridge and claude," with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anthropic" title="Anthropic" id="mw9A">Anthropic's</a> <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claude_(language_model)" title="Claude (language model)" class="mw-redirect" id="mw9Q">Claude</a> being used in the coding process. This led a user to post a GitHub issue called <i id="mw9g">Please Do Not Vibe Fuck Up This Software</i>, which spread to other social media sites like <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reddit" title="Reddit" id="mw9w">Reddit</a>, leading to debates over AI-generated code getting accepted into critical open source infrastructure.<sup about="#mwt95" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:02_26-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":02"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:02-26"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:02-26" id="mw-A"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw-Q"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-g">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mw-w">]</span></span></a></sup> In response, Tridge published a blog post called <i id="mw_A">rsync and outrage</i>, in which he states he used AI to add test suites and "defence-in-depth hardening techniques" to the code.<sup about="#mwt98" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-27" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-27"}}'><a href="#cite_note-27" id="mw_Q"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mw_g"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mw_w">[</span>27<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQA">]</span></span></a></sup> Tridge also stated that <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenBSD" title="OpenBSD" id="mwAQE">OpenBSD's</a> openrsync, an alternate implementation of rsync that some users were planning to switch to, fails the AI-assisted test suite; this statement was criticized by <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OSNews" title="OSNews" id="mwAQI">OSNews</a> editor Thom Holwerda as being "childish and unnecessary, and reeks of insecurity".<sup about="#mwt99" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:02_26-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":02"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:02-26" id="mwAQM"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQU">[</span>26<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQY">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt102" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-28" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-28"}}'><a href="#cite_note-28" id="mwAQc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAQg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQk">[</span>28<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAQo">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="5" id="mwAQs" aria-labelledby="Code_maintainability_and_technical_debt"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Code_maintainability_and_technical_debt">Code maintainability and technical debt</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Code maintainability and technical debt"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwAQw">Vibe coding has the potential of making code harder to maintain in the longer term, leading to technical debt.</p>

<p id="mwAQ0">In early 2025, GitClear published the results of a longitudinal analysis of 211 million lines of code changes from 2020 to 2024. They found that the volume of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Code_refactoring" title="Code refactoring" id="mwAQ4">code refactoring</a> dropped from 25% of changed lines in 2021 to under 10% by 2024, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Code_duplication" title="Code duplication" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAQ8">code duplication</a> increased approximately four times in volume, copy-pasted code exceeded moved code for the first time in two decades, and code churn (prematurely merged code getting rewritten shortly after merging) nearly doubled.<sup about="#mwt105" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-29" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-29"}}'><a href="#cite_note-29" id="mwARA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARI">[</span>29<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt106" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:1_23-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:1-23" id="mwARQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwARU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARY">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwARc">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwARg">In September 2025, <i id="mwARk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fast_Company" title="Fast Company" id="mwARo">Fast Company</a></i> reported that the "vibe coding hangover" is upon us, with senior software engineers citing "<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Development_hell" title="Development hell" id="mwARs">development hell</a>" when working with AI-generated code.<sup about="#mwt109" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-30" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-30"}}'><a href="#cite_note-30" id="mwARw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAR0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAR4">[</span>30<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAR8">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="6" id="mwASA" aria-labelledby="Task_complexity_and_developer_productivity"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Task_complexity_and_developer_productivity">Task complexity and developer productivity</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: Task complexity and developer productivity"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwASE">Generative AI is capable of handling simple tasks like basic algorithms<link rel="mw:PageProp/Category" href="./Category:Wikipedia_articles_needing_clarification_from_June_2026" about="#mwt110" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Clarify","href":"./Template:Clarify"},"params":{"reason":{"wt":"Which algorithms?"},"date":{"wt":"June 2026"}},"i":0}}]}'/><sup class="noprint Inline-Template " style="margin-left:0.1em; white-space:nowrap;" about="#mwt110" id="mwASM"><span typeof="mw:Entity">[</span><i><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia:Please_clarify" title="Wikipedia:Please clarify"><span title="Which algorithms? (June 2026)">clarification needed</span></a></i><span typeof="mw:Entity">]</span></sup>. However, such systems struggle with more novel, complex coding problems like projects involving multiple files, poorly documented libraries, or safety-critical code.<sup about="#mwt113" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_31-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-:0-31"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-31" id="mwASQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwASU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASY">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwASc">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwASg">In July 2025, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/METR" title="METR" id="mwASk">METR</a>, an organization that evaluates <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frontier_model" title="Frontier model" class="mw-redirect" id="mwASo">frontier models</a>, ran a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Randomized_controlled_trial" title="Randomized controlled trial" id="mwASs">randomized controlled trial</a> in which 16 experienced open-source developers completed 246 tasks in mature repositories. In that setting, allowing early-2025 AI tools increased completion time by 19%, although participants had predicted before the tasks that AI would reduce completion time by 24%.<sup about="#mwt116" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-32" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-32"}}'><a href="#cite_note-32" id="mwASw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAS0"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS4">[</span>32<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAS8">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt119" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-33" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-33"}}'><a href="#cite_note-33" id="mwATA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATE"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATI">[</span>33<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATM">]</span></span></a></sup><sup about="#mwt120" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:1_23-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:1-23" id="mwATQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATY">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATc">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="7" id="mwATg" aria-labelledby="Challenges_with_debugging"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Challenges_with_debugging">Challenges with debugging</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Challenges with debugging"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwATk">LLMs generate code dynamically, and the structure of such code may be subject to variation.<sup about="#mwt123" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-FastCompany2025_34-0" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":"FastCompany2025"},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-FastCompany2025-34"}}'><a href="#cite_note-FastCompany2025-34" id="mwATo"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwATs"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwATw">[</span>34<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAT0">]</span></span></a></sup> In addition, since the developer did not write the code, the developer may struggle to understand its syntax and concepts.<sup about="#mwt124" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-:0_31-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{"name":":0"}}'><a href="#cite_note-:0-31" id="mwAT4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAT8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUA">[</span>31<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUE">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAUI">In May 2026, <i id="mwAUM">The Wall Street Journal</i> reported on criticism from Mario Zechner and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armin_Ronacher" title="Armin Ronacher" id="mwAUQ">Armin Ronacher</a>, engineers behind the Pi coding harness inside the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/OpenClaw" title="OpenClaw" id="mwAUU">OpenClaw</a> AI agent system. They warned of a looming "vibe slop" crisis. They opined that companies are trading near-term productivity for longer-term problems, including buggy software, service outages, security vulnerabilities and increased <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technical_debt" title="Technical debt" id="mwAUY">technical debt</a>. Zechner was quoted saying "You have infrastructure that’s falling apart, and you have software that’s now very, very buggy compared to before. We can play this game for a couple more months, or maybe even years, but eventually it will catch up to us."<sup about="#mwt127" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-35" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-35"}}'><a href="#cite_note-35" id="mwAUc"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAUg"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUk">[</span>35<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAUo">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="8" id="mwAUs" aria-labelledby="Impact_on_open-source_software"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Impact_on_open-source_software">Impact on open-source software</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Impact on open-source software"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div><p id="mwAUw">
In January 2026, a paper authored by experts from several universities titled "Vibe Coding Kills Open Source"<sup about="#mwt130" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-36" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-36"}}'><a href="#cite_note-36" id="mwAU0"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAU4"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAU8">[</span>36<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVA">]</span></span></a></sup> argued that vibe coding has negative impact on the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-source_software" title="Open-source software" id="mwAVE">open-source software</a> ecosystem. The authors say that increased vibe coding reduces user engagement with open-source maintainers, which has hidden costs for said maintainers. Speaking with <i id="mwAVI"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Register" title="The Register" id="mwAVM">The Register</a></i> about their paper, the authors argued:<sup about="#mwt133" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-37" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-37"}}'><a href="#cite_note-37" id="mwAVQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAVU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVY">[</span>37<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAVc">]</span></span></a></sup></p><blockquote id="mwAVg"><p id="mwAVk">"Vibe coding raises productivity by lowering the cost of using and building on existing code, but it also weakens the user engagement through which many maintainers earn returns," the authors argue. "When OSS is monetized only through direct user engagement, greater adoption of vibe coding lowers entry and sharing, reduces the availability and quality of OSS, and reduces welfare despite higher productivity."</p></blockquote><p id="mwAVo">They added that revenue is not the only thing that may be affected by this trend, as open-source software maintainers traditionally also get non-tangible benefits from their work, such as community recognition, reputation, and job prospects.</p>

<p id="mwAVs">Maya Posch, explaining the paper's claims on <i id="mwAVw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hackaday" title="Hackaday" id="mwAV0">Hackaday</a></i>, expanded on the explanation. She pointed out that the mechanism for vibe coding lowering harmony with open-source projects is the homogenization of software development; language models will gravitate towards large and established libraries that appear frequently in their training dataset, removing the organic selection process of libraries and tooling and making it harder for newer open-source tools to get noticed. She also pointed out that language models will not submit useful bug reports to the maintainers, or be aware of potential issues.<sup about="#mwt136" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-38" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-38"}}'><a href="#cite_note-38" id="mwAV4"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAV8"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWA">[</span>38<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWE">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwAWI">In February 2026, GitHub acknowledged an increase lower quality AI-generated contributions overwhelming open source maintainers, describing the phenomenon as an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eternal_September" title="Eternal September" id="mwAWM">Eternal September</a> for open source. They cited examples of projects already affected, including the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CURL" title="CURL" id="mwAWQ">cURL</a> project ending its bug bounty program after AI-generated security reports multiplied, and some projects, such as Ghostty, moving to invitation-only contribution models. The platform introduced new maintainer controls, including the ability to restrict pull request creation to collaborators, temporary limits on repository activity as a means of crowd control, and communication tools such as banners and pinned messages in issues.<sup about="#mwt139" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-39" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-39"}}'><a href="#cite_note-39" id="mwAWU"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwAWY"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWc">[</span>39<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwAWg">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="9" id="mwAWk" aria-labelledby="See_also"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="See_also">See also</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: See also"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="10" id="mwAXU" aria-labelledby="References"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="References">References</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Vibe_coding&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: References"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

Retrieved <span class="nowrap" id="mwAY8">3 June</span> 2025</span>. <q id="mwAZA">The technique, enabled by large language models (LLMs) from companies like OpenAI and Anthropic, has attracted attention for potentially lowering the barrier to entry for software creation. <li about="#cite_note-The_Times_of_India-2025-2" id="cite_note-The_Times_of_India-2025-2" data-mw-footnote-number="2"><span rel="mw:referencedBy" class="mw-cite-backlink" id="mwAZI"><a href="#cite_ref-The_Times_of_India-2025_2-0" id="mwAZM"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAZQ">1</span></a> <a href="#cite_ref-The_Times_of_India-2025_2-1" id="mwAZU"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAZY">2</span></a> <a href="#cite_ref-The_Times_of_India-2025_2-2" id="mwAZc"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAZg">3</span></a></span> <span id="mw-reference-text-cite_note-The_Times_of_India-2025-2" class="mw-reference-text reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" about="#mwt6" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwAZk" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Citation/CS1/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"cite news ","href":"./Template:Cite_news"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"2 March 2025"},"title":{"wt":"What is &apos;vibe code&apos;? Former Tesla AI director Andrej Karpathy defines a new era in AI-driven development"</a>. <i id="mwAZw"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Times_of_India" title="The Times of India" id="mwAZ0">The Times of India</a></i>. 2 March 2025. <a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://web.archive.org/web/20250304014054/https://timesofindia.indiatimes.com/technology/tech-news/what-is-vibe-coding-former-tesla-ai-director-andrej-karpathy-defines-a-new-era-in-ai-driven-development/articleshow/118659724.cms" class="external text" id="mwAZ4">Archived</a> from the original on 4 March 2025<span class="reference-accessdate" id="mwAZ8">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap" id="mwAaA">3 June</span> 2025</span>. <q id="mwAaE">Karpathy's "vibe coding" is a recognition of how sophisticated AI systems have evolved. In describing on X (formerly Twitter), he added that LLMs, like the Cursor Composer with Sonnet, are advancing to a degree that nearly eliminates the use of traditional coding mechanisms. Describing his own experience, Karpathy explained how he converses with AI tools almost in a passive manner—merely talking to them and having the AI handle the rest. This method eliminates manually typing code as well as keeping track of all the minute information in the program.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=article&amp;rft.jtitle=The+Times+of+India&amp;rft.atitle=What+is+%27vibe+code%27%3F+Former+Tesla+AI+director+Andrej+Karpathy+defines+a+new+era+in+AI-driven+development&amp;rft.date=2025-03-02&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Ftimesofindia.indiatimes.com%2Ftechnology%2Ftech-news%2Fwhat-is-vibe-coding-former-tesla-ai-director-andrej-karpathy-defines-a-new-era-in-ai-driven-development%2Farticleshow%2F118659724.cms&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AVibe+coding" class="Z3988" about="#mwt6" id="mwAaI"></span></span></li>
<li about="#cite_note-merriam-webster-3" id="cite_note-merriam-webster-3" data-mw-footnote-number="3"><span class="mw-cite-backlink" id="mwAaM"><a href="#cite_ref-merriam-webster_3-0" rel="mw:referencedBy" id="mwAaQ"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAaU">↑</span></a></span> <span id="mw-reference-text-cite_note-merriam-webster-3" class="mw-reference-text reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" about="#mwt9" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwAaY" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Citation/CS1/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"cite web ","href":"./Template:Cite_web"},"params":{"date":{"wt":"8 March 2025"},"title":{"wt":"vibe coding"},"url":{"wt":"https://www.merriam-webster.com/slang/vibe-coding"},"access-date":{"wt":"2 June 2025"},"work":{"wt":"Slang &amp; Trending"},"publisher":{"wt":"Merriam-Webster"},"language":{"wt":"en"},"quote":{"wt":"Vibe coding (also written as vibecoding) (Vibecode/Vibecoder) is a recently-coined term for the practice of writing code, making web pages, or creating apps, by just telling an AI program what you want, and letting it create the product for you. In vibe coding the coder does not need to understand how or why the code works, and often will have to accept that a certain number of bugs and glitches will be present. The verb form of the word is vibe code."}},"i":0}}]}'/><cite class="citation web cs1" about="#mwt9" id="mwAac"><a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://www.merriam-webster.com/slang/vibe-coding" class="external text" id="mwAag">"vibe coding"</a>. <i id="mwAak">Slang &amp; Trending</i>. Merriam-Webster. 8 March 2025<span class="reference-accessdate" id="mwAao">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap" id="mwAas">2 June</span> 2025</span>. <q id="mwAaw">Vibe coding (also written as vibecoding) (Vibecode/Vibecoder) is a recently-coined term for the practice of writing code, making web pages, or creating apps, by just telling an AI program what you want, and letting it create the product for you. In vibe coding the coder does not need to understand how or why the code works, and often will have to accept that a certain number of bugs and glitches will be present. The verb form of the word is vibe code.</q></cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Ajournal&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.jtitle=Slang+%26+Trending&amp;rft.atitle=vibe+coding&amp;rft.date=2025-03-08&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.merriam-webster.com%2Fslang%2Fvibe-coding&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AVibe+coding" class="Z3988" about="#mwt9" id="mwAa0"></span></span></li>

<li about="#cite_note-Chowdhury-2025-7" id="cite_note-Chowdhury-2025-7" data-mw-footnote-number="7"><span rel="mw:referencedBy" class="mw-cite-backlink" id="mwAcs"><a href="#cite_ref-Chowdhury-2025_7-0" id="mwAcw"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAc0">1</span></a> <a href="#cite_ref-Chowdhury-2025_7-1" id="mwAc4"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAc8">2</span></a> <a href="#cite_ref-Chowdhury-2025_7-2" id="mwAdA"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAdE">3</span></a></span> <span id="mw-reference-text-cite_note-Chowdhury-2025-7" class="mw-reference-text reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" about="#mwt21" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwAdI" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Citation/CS1/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"cite web ","href":"./Template:Cite_web"},"params":{"last1":{"wt":"Chowdhury"},"first1":{"wt":"Hasan"},"last2":{"wt":"Mann"},"first2":{"wt":"Jyoti"},"date":{"wt":"13 February 2025"},"title":{"wt":"Silicon Valley&apos;s next act: bringing &apos;vibe coding&apos; <li about="#cite_note-Karpathy-X-post-8" id="cite_note-Karpathy-X-post-8" data-mw-footnote-number="8"><span class="mw-cite-backlink" id="mwAdo"><a href="#cite_ref-Karpathy-X-post_8-0" rel="mw:referencedBy" id="mwAds"><span class="mw-linkback-text" id="mwAdw">↑</span></a></span> <span id="mw-reference-text-cite_note-Karpathy-X-post-8" class="mw-reference-text reference-text"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1333433106" about="#mwt29" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles mw:Transclusion" id="mwAd0" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Citation/CS1/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""},"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Cite tweet ","href":"./Template:Cite_tweet"},"params":{"first":{"wt":"Andrej"},"last":{"wt":"Karpathy"},"title":{"wt":"There&apos;s a new kind of coding I call \"vibe coding\", where you fully give in to the vibes, embrace exponentials, and forget that the code even exists. It&apos;s possible because the LLMs (e.g. Cursor Composer w Sonnet) are getting too good. Also I just talk to Composer with SuperWhisper so I barely even touch the keyboard. I ask for the dumbest things like \"decrease the padding on the sidebar by half\" because I&apos;m too lazy to find it. I \"Accept All\" always, I don&apos;t read the diffs anymore. When I get error messages I just copy paste them in with no comment, usually that fixes it. The code grows beyond my usual comprehension, I&apos;d have to really read through it for a while. Sometimes the LLMs can&apos;t fix a bug so I just work around it or ask for random changes until it goes away. It&apos;s not too bad for throwaway weekend projects, but still quite amusing. I&apos;m building a project or webapp, but it&apos;s not really coding - I just see stuff, say stuff, run stuff, and copy paste stuff, and it mostly works."},"author-link":{"wt":"Andrej Karpathy"},"user":{"wt":"karpathy"},"number":{"wt":"1886192184808149383"},"access-date":{"wt":"16 September 2025"}},"i":0}}]}'/><cite id="CITEREFKarpathy2025" class="citation web cs1" about="#mwt29"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrej_Karpathy" title="Andrej Karpathy" id="mwAd4">Karpathy, Andrej [@karpathy]</a> (2 February 2025). <a rel="mw:ExtLink nofollow" href="https://x.com/karpathy/status/1886192184808149383" class="external text" id="mwAd8">"There's a new kind of coding I call "vibe coding", where you fully give in to the vibes, embrace exponentials, and forget that the code even exists. It's possible because the LLMs (e.g. Cursor Composer w Sonnet) are getting too good. Also I just talk to Composer with SuperWhisper so I barely even touch the keyboard. I ask for the dumbest things like "decrease the padding on the sidebar by half" because I'm too lazy to find it. I "Accept All" always, I don't read the diffs anymore. When I get error messages I just copy paste them in with no comment, usually that fixes it. The code grows beyond my usual comprehension, I'd have to really read through it for a while. Sometimes the LLMs can't fix a bug so I just work around it or ask for random changes until it goes away. It's not too bad for throwaway weekend projects, but still quite amusing. I'm building a project or webapp, but it's not really coding - I just see stuff, say stuff, run stuff, and copy paste stuff, and it mostly works"</a> (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tweet_(social_media)" title="Tweet (social media)" id="mwAeA">Tweet</a>)<span class="reference-accessdate" id="mwAeE">. Retrieved <span class="nowrap" id="mwAeI">16 September</span> 2025</span> <span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwAeM">–</span> via <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/X_(formerly_Twitter)" title="X (formerly Twitter)" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAeQ">X (formerly Twitter)</a>.</cite><span title="ctx_ver=Z39.88-2004&amp;rft_val_fmt=info%3Aofi%2Ffmt%3Akev%3Amtx%3Abook&amp;rft.genre=unknown&amp;rft.btitle=There%27s+a+new+kind+of+coding+I+call+%22vibe+coding%22%2C+where+you+fully+give+in+to+the+vibes%2C+embrace+exponentials%2C+and+forget+that+the+code+even+exists.+It%27s+possible+because+the+LLMs+%28e.g.+Cursor+Composer+w+Sonnet%29+are+getting+too+good.+Also+I+just+talk+to+Composer+with+SuperWhisper+so+I+barely+even+touch+the+keyboard.+I+ask+for+the+dumbest+things+like+%22decrease+the+padding+on+the+sidebar+by+half%22+because+I%27m+too+lazy+to+find+it.+I+%22Accept+All%22+always%2C+I+don%27t+read+the+diffs+anymore.+When+I+get+error+messages+I+just+copy+paste+them+in+with+no+comment%2C+usually+that+fixes+it.+The+code+grows+beyond+my+usual+comprehension%2C+I%27d+have+to+really+read+through+it+for+a+while.+Sometimes+the+LLMs+can%27t+fix+a+bug+so+I+just+work+around+it+or+ask+for+random+changes+until+it+goes+away.+It%27s+not+too+bad+for+throwaway+weekend+projects%2C+but+still+quite+amusing.+I%27m+building+a+project+or+webapp%2C+but+it%27s+not+really+coding+-+I+just+see+stuff%2C+say+stuff%2C+run+stuff%2C+and+copy+paste+stuff%2C+and+it+mostly+works.&amp;rft.date=2025-02-02&amp;rft.aulast=Karpathy&amp;rft.aufirst=Andrej&amp;rft_id=https%3A%2F%2Fx.com%2Fkarpathy%2Fstatus%2F1886192184808149383&amp;rfr_id=info%3Asid%2Fen.wikipedia.org%3AVibe+coding" class="Z3988" about="#mwt29" id="mwAeU"></span></span></li>
